We can either:\n  (a) before factoring diagonal block, update it using previously computed L columns: A[kb:kb+ib, kb:kb+ib] -= L[kb:kb+ib, 0:kb] * L[kb:kb+ib,0:kb]^T (SYRK), i.e., panel of width kb? For right-looking blocked algorithm, trailing matrix is updated each step, so diagonal block already has contributions from previous panels. In right-looking, after each panel update, trailing A22 -= L21 L21^T includes diagonal block. So when we get to block kb, A[kb:kb+ib,kb:kb+ib] is already updated. Then factor the block using only within-block prior columns. Yes right-looking.\n```\n\nSo algorithm:\n```\nfor kb in 0..n step nb:\n  ib = min(nb, n-kb)\n  // factor diagonal block A[kb:kb+ib,kb:kb+ib] lower col-major\n  for jj in 0..ib-1:\n     j=kb+jj\n     sum=0; for l=kb; l<j; l++ sum += A[j+l*n]^2;  // only within block\n     val=A[j+j*n]-sum\n     if val<=0 error\n     A[j+j*n]=sqrt(val)\n     for ii=jj+1..ib-1:\n        i=kb+ii\n        s=0; for l=kb; l<j; l++ s += A[i+l*n]*A[j+l*n]\n        A[i+j*n] = (A[i+j*n]-s)/A[j+j*n]\n  if kb+ib < n:\n     m = n-(kb+ib)\n     // extract panel A[kb+ib:n, kb:kb+ib] into row-major L21[m][ib]\n     // maybe allocate workspace once max m*ib\n     parallel for i=0..m-1:\n        for jj=0..ib-1: L21[i*ib+jj] = A[(kb+ib+i) + (kb+jj)*n]\n     // triangular solve L11^T * X = A21 -> X = L21 * inv(L11^T)\n     // for each row i (independent):\n     // for jj=0..ib-1:\n     //   s=0; for l=0..jj-1 s += L21[i*ib+l]*A[(kb+l)+(kb+jj)*n]\n     //   L21[i*ib+jj] = (L21[i*ib+jj]-s)/A[(kb+jj)+(kb+jj)*n]\n     parallel for i\n     // copy L21 back to A panel columns (needed for result)\n     parallel for i for jj A[(kb+ib+i)+(kb+jj)*n] = L21[...]\n     // trailing update A[(kb+ib):n, (kb+ib):n] -= L21 L21^T lower\n     parallel for i (row in trailing) 0..m-1:\n        // local row index ii = i, global row r=kb+ib+i\n        // compute for j=0..i (lower within trailing): dot over jj\n        for j=0..i:\n            s=0; for jj=0..ib-1 s += L21[i*ib+jj]*L21[j*ib+jj]\n            A[(kb+ib+i)+(kb+ib+j)*n] -= s\n```\n\nThis trailing update only lower triangle and matches m^2/2*ib. 64 : 128;\n    const size_t max_panel = std::min(n, nb);\n\n    std::vector<double> panel(static_cast<size_t>(n) * max_panel);\n\n    for (size_t kb = 0; kb < n; kb += nb) {\n        const size_t ib = std::min(nb, n - kb);\n\n        // Factor the diagonal block A[kb:kb+ib, kb:kb+ib] in place.\n        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < ib; ++jj) {\n            const size_t j = kb + jj;\n            const double val = A[j + j * n];\n            if (val <= 0.0) {\n                printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n                return false;\n            }\n            const double d = std::sqrt(val);\n            const double inv_d = 1.0 / d;\n            A[j + j * n] = d;\n\n            for (size_t ii = jj + 1; ii < ib; ++ii) {\n                A[(kb + ii) + j * n] *= inv_d;\n            }\n\n            for (size_t ll = jj + 1; ll < ib; ++ll) {\n                const size_t l = kb + ll;\n                const double fl = A[l + j * n];\n                const double* col_j = A.data() + j * n;\n                double* col_l = A.data() + l * n;\n                for (size_t ii = ll; ii < ib; ++ii) {\n                    const size_t i = kb + ii;\n                    col_l[i] -= col_j[i] * fl;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        const size_t r0 = kb + ib;\n        if (r0 < n) {\n            const size_t m = n - r0;\n\n            // Copy the panel below the diagonal block into a contiguous\n            // column-major working buffer (panel[jj*m + i] == L21(i,jj)).\n            #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t jj = 0; jj < ib; ++jj) {\n                const double* src = A.data() + r0 + (kb + jj) * n;\n                double* dst = panel.data() + jj * m;\n                #pragma omp simd\n                for (size_t i = 0; i < m; ++i) {\n                    dst[i] = src[i];\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Solve L11^T * X = A21 for the whole panel. So only up to 10 entries valid. We can compute all 16 and conditionally write lower entries; extra work small (diagonal tiles only ~nt vs nt^2/2). Or special-case diagonal microtiles. Simpler: compute accumulators for all 16, then write using condition r>=c; for offdiag all true. But loops over j from j0 to j1 and i from i0 to i1; for offdiag i0>j1 so all valid. For diagonal tile, j1 maybe > i0; with i_blk and j_blk, if j_blk>i_blk skip. Use nested i_blk from i0 step4, j_blk from j0 step 4 up to min(j1, i_blk+4?) Actually for a given i_blk, only j <= i, so j_blk up to min(j1, i_blk+4). For j_blk < i_blk full; for j_blk==i_blk lower microtriangle. Good.\n\nPseudo:\n```\nfor (size_t ib0=i0; ib0<i1; ib0+=4) {\n  size_t imax=min(ib0+4,i1);\n  size_t jmax_tile = min(j1, ib0+4); // j up to i range\n  for (size_t jb0=j0; jb0<jmax_tile; jb0+=4) {\n     size_t jmax=min(jb0+4, jmax_tile);\n     // ensure jb0 <= ib0; since j0..; if jb0 > ib0 skip not needed\n     double acc[4][4]={0};\n     for kk:\n        double a0=bk[ib0], a1 = ib0+1<imax?..., ...\n        b0...\n        acc00 += a0*b0; ...\n     // write valid entries i>=j\n     for ii=0; ii<imax-ib0; ++ii\n       for jj=0; jj<jmax-jb0; ++jj\n         if (ib0+ii >= jb0+jj)\n            A[(r0+jb0+jj)+(r0+ib0+ii)*n]? Wait C index (row i, col j): A[(r0+i) + (r0+j)*n].\n```\nCareful: earlier c = A + (r0+j)*n and c[r0+i] = index (r0+j)*n + r0+i, which is column j, row i. So for acc[ii][jj] (row i, col j), subtract at `A[(r0+i) + (r0+j)*n]`. Good.\n\nBut the microtile loops order: for each i_blk, j_blk. Need `bk` per kk inside; if kk outer then accumulate all? For register accumulation, kk must be innermost per microtile, so loops i_blk, j_blk, kk. That means for each microtile, read bk chunks over all ib. B rows length m; for each microtile i_blk,j_blk, reads 8 doubles per kk, total. B traffic = per microtile ib*8 doubles; #microtiles ~ (m/4)*(m/8)? Actually lower triangle ~m^2/(2*16) microtiles * ib*8*8 bytes = m^2/32*ib*64 =2*m^2*ib bytes per panel, vs tile schedule with ts=64 gave m^2*ib*8/64=0.125*m^2*ib bytes. Microtile 4x4 increases B traffic 16x! Because for each 4x4, read a and b vectors separately; no reuse across neighboring microtiles. But B fits in cache; L3/L2 bandwidth maybe okay, but better to use larger macro tile and accumulate in registers? Standard GEMM uses block over K in L1 and reuses A/B across multiple microtiles with register blocking NR/MR. To retain A/B reuse, process a macro tile with all kk but multiple microtiles sharing bk loads: e.g., for a fixed i block (4 rows) and all j in macro tile, loop kk outer and keep accumulators for say 4 columns x 4 rows? That's 4x4 only; to reuse a0..a3 across multiple j microblocks, we'd need accumulators for 4 rows x 16 columns (64 regs) too many. Standard microkernel MR=4, NR=4 stores accumulators in registers and loads A/B from L1; B traffic is high but L1/L2 bandwidth handles. We can increase MR/NR to 8x4 or 4x8 to reduce B traffic per flop? For microkernel MR x NR, per kk reads MR+NR, does MR*NR FMAs. Intensity ~ MR*NR/(MR+NR). 4x4 ->16/8=2 FMA per double = 4 flops/8bytes=0.5. 8x4 ->32/12=2.67 (0.67), 8x8 ->64/16=4 (1 flop/byte). 8x8 requires 64 accumulator registers + 16 inputs, 80, feasible in AVX2 (16 regs) not scalar; compiler would spill. Scalar registers 16 x87/SSE, can't. But compiler with AVX2 can use 16 YMM regs =64 doubles if packed. Hard.\n\nOur current rank-1 tile approach reads B once per kk per j tile (good) but writes C each kk. Maybe use a hybrid: process KC block of kk (e.g., 16) and keep C tile? For a tile, dot version B traffic = for each (i,j) read 2*ib doubles = ts^2*ib*16 bytes; rank-1 B traffic = ib*2*ts*8 (much less) but C traffic = ts^2*ib*16 (read+write) same magnitude. So both have same total L1 traffic ~ts^2*ib*16 bytes, except rank-1 reads/writes C (read+write =16 bytes) vs dot reads two B =16 bytes. Same. But dot version uses vectorized dot over ib (length128) and C write once; B values are reused across tiles? For each (i,j) dot reads B rows i and j, row i reused for all j in tile? Could cache. Maybe dot version with vectorization could be faster than rank-1 because loads are contiguous along kk? B is column-major (Bt rows = panel columns, each row length m). For a fixed row i, B(i,kk)=Bt[kk*m+i], stride m across kk; not contiguous. So dot is strided -> poor. If we stored panel row-major B (m x ib), dot over kk contiguous for each row, and B row cached. This might be much faster due to vectorized dot (2 loads per FMA) and C write amortized. B traffic same magnitude as rank-1 C traffic but dot loops vectorize nicely. Could be ~2x better. The factor is converted\n// back to row-major storage (with a zeroed upper triangle) before returning.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    // Block size: 64 for small matrices, 128 for larger ones.\n    const size_t nb = (n < 512) ? 64 : 128;\n    const size_t max_panel = std::min(n, nb);\n\n    // Reused panel workspace. panel[jj * m + i] holds L21(i, jj).\n    std::vector<double> panel(static_cast<size_t>(n) * max_panel);\n\n    for (size_t kb = 0; The factor is converted\n// back to row-major storage (with a zeroed upper triangle) before returning.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    // Block size: 64 for small matrices, 128 for larger ones.\n    const size_t nb = (n < 512) ? 64 : 128;\n    const size_t max_panel = std::min(n, nb);\n\n    // Reused panel workspace. panel[jj * m + i] holds L21(i, jj).\n    std::vector<double> panel(static_cast<size_t>(n) * max_panel);\n\n    bool positive = true;\n\n    // A single persistent parallel region avoids repeatedly starting worker\n    // threads for the many short phases of the factorization.\n    #pragma omp parallel shared(A, panel, n, nb, positive)\n    {\n        const size_t team = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_num_threads());\n\n        for (size_t kb = 0;
